configuration persists. Return and downward direction then form a single reversal process in which what stood is placed back into a lower state.","ayah_refs":["95:4","95:5"]},{"parent_channel":"Functional Repair and Substitution","parent_semantic_invariant":"A defective or absent function is restored by activation, compatible replacement, restraint, or exact repair.","parent_surprising_reach":"Creation and good action open into a technical scene of operating parts, substituting equivalents, arresting failure, and securing a finished repair.","key":"B","name":"Compatible Replacement and Restoration","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"One item stands in for another, fits its intended place, and restores the prior arrangement without delay.","active_motifs":"standing in another's place `ق و م:B007/m01`, replacement by another `غ ي ر:B003/m01`, return to owner or place `ر د د:B001/m02`, functional suitability `ص ل ح:B003/m01`, enactment without delay `ك ذ ب:B005/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:4 `تقويم`; 95:5 `رددناه`; 95:6 `الصالحات`، `غير`; 95:7 `يكذبك`","synthesis":"Substitution is not mere exchange: the replacement must fit the vacant function and be returned into the operative position. Immediacy closes the mechanism by preventing the restored function from remaining suspended.","ayah_refs":["95:4","95:5","95:6","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Functional Repair and Substitution","parent_semantic_invariant":"A defective or absent function is restored by activation, compatible replacement, restraint, or exact repair.","parent_surprising_reach":"Creation and good action open into a technical scene of operating parts, substituting equivalents, arresting failure, and securing a finished repair.","key":"C","name":"Restraint, Repulsion, and Repair","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Harm or corruption is stopped, pushed away, and replaced with a sound condition.","active_motifs":"restraint against corruption `ح ك م:B001/m01`, repulsion and prevention `ر د د:B002/m01`, repair after فساد `ص ل ح:B001/m02`, corrective substitution `غ ي ر:B003/m02`, repair of equipment `غ ي ر:B001/m02`","ayah_anchors":"95:5 `رددناه`; 95:6 `الصالحات`، `غير`; 95:8 `أحكم`","synthesis":"Restraint blocks the damaging motion, repulsion removes it from its course, and repair reinstates sound operation. Corrective substitution supplies the practical means by which the harmful state is exchanged for a serviceable one.","ayah_refs":["95:5","95:6","95:8"]},{"parent_channel":"Judgment, Authority, and Truth","parent_semantic_invariant":"Claims, conduct, and obligations are entrusted to an authority that distinguishes truth, assigns judgment, and brings a final account.","parent_surprising_reach":"Final judgment reaches into arbitration, testimony, counterfeit rejection, fabricated speech, and the contrast between penetrating and weak counsel.","key":"C","name":"Fabrication and Rejection","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A false account is manufactured, exposed, and refused like a defective or counterfeit item.","active_motifs":"falsehood opposed to truth `ك ذ ب:B001/m01`, fabricated speech `خ ل ق:B007/m01`, rejection of error or counterfeit `ر د د:B003/m01`, discrediting a speaker `ك ذ ب:B002/m02`","ayah_anchors":"95:4 `خلقنا`; 95:5 `رددناه`; 95:7 `يكذبك`","synthesis":"Fabrication gives falsehood the shape of a made object, while rejection treats it as something inspected and returned. The social act of discrediting the speaker completes the movement from manufactured claim to refused claim.","ayah_refs":["95:4","95:5","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Work, Exchange, and Recompense","parent_semantic_invariant":"Deliberate labor enters relations of wage, debt, valuation, restitution, livelihood, and release.","parent_surprising_reach":"The unending reward scene expands into labor markets, credit, pricing, equal weights, rescission, public office, bondage, and release without ransom.","key":"B","name":"Debt, Dealing, and Rescission","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Parties enter a deferred obligation, transact with one another, settle conflict, and may reclaim or unwind the exchange.","active_motifs":"debt and credit sale `د ي ن:B003/m01`, interpersonal dealing `ع م ل:B005/m01`, repossession and rescission `ر د د:B004/m01`, negotiated settlement `ص ل ح:B002/m01`, assessed compensation `ح ك م:B002/m02`","ayah_anchors":"95:5 `رددناه`; 95:6 `عملوا`، `الصالحات`; 95:7 `الدين`; 95:8 `أحكم`","synthesis":"Debt creates the continuing obligation, dealing establishes the relation between parties, and settlement governs disagreement within it. Rescission and assessed compensation provide two different closures: reversing the transaction or fixing what one party owes.","ayah_refs":["95:5","95:6","95:7","95:8"]},{"parent_channel":"Work, Exchange, and Recompense","parent_semantic_invariant":"Deliberate labor enters relations of wage, debt, valuation, restitution, livelihood, and release.","parent_surprising_reach":"The unending reward scene expands into labor markets, credit, pricing, equal weights, rescission, public office, bondage, and release without ransom.","key":"E","name":"Bondage, Restitution, and Unransomed Release","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A person is held under coercive ownership, then either restored through return or released without a counterpayment.","active_motifs":"subjugation and enslavement `د ي ن:B004/m01`, release without ransom `م ن ن:B004/m01`, recovery and return `ر د د:B004/m02`","ayah_anchors":"95:5 `رددناه`; 95:6 `ممنون`; 95:7 `الدين`","synthesis":"Subjugation establishes control over the person, recovery reverses possession, and gratuitous release ends captivity without equivalent exchange. The scene turns the language of debt and payment into a contrast between priced return and unpriced freedom.","ayah_refs":["95:5","95:6","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Faith, Worship, and Moral Character","parent_semantic_invariant":"Inner disposition aligns with worship, belief, upright conduct, benefaction, and the possibility of religious return or rupture.","parent_surprising_reach":"Religious alignment is rendered as inward character, straightness, beneficial action, apostasy, divine favor, and the destructive social act of recounting a favor.","key":"C","name":"Apostasy and Religious Return","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A person reverses religious allegiance, leaving an upright order for its contrary.","active_motifs":"apostatic return `ر د د:B005/m01`, religious obedience `د ي ن:B001/m01`, straight alignment `ق و م:B008/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:4 `تقويم`; 95:5 `رددناه`; 95:7 `الدين`","synthesis":"Religious straightness supplies the initial alignment, while apostatic return gives reversal a specific social and doctrinal form. The same root that returns a thing to a prior state can therefore mark a departure from the adopted religious order.","ayah_refs":["95:4","95:5","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Boundary, Relative Position, and Separation","parent_semantic_invariant":"Entities are located by edges, near and far relations, facing and trailing sides, and movement across a lower or outer boundary.","parent_surprising_reach":"Spatial language reaches from courtyards and distant places into spear anatomy, the human-facing side of an object, posteriority, and a lower bodily outlet.","key":"B","name":"Facing Side, Forepart, and Posteriority","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A body or implement is oriented by the side facing a person, its working forepart, its lower line, and what comes behind.","active_motifs":"human-facing inner side `ء ن س:B004/m01`, spear forepart `ع م ل:B009/m01`, posterior or subsequent position `ب ع د:B002/m01`, lower line `س ف ل:B001/m01`, leeward side `س ف ل:B003/m01`, aligned edge `ط و ر:B001/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:2 `طور`; 95:4 `الإنسان`; 95:5 `أسفل`; 95:6 `عملوا`; 95:7 `بعد`","synthesis":"The human-facing side fixes orientation from the participant's viewpoint, while the spear's forepart supplies a functional front. Lower, leeward, and posterior positions complete a directional frame that works across bodies, tools, wind, and sequences.","ayah_refs":["95:2","95:4","95:5","95:6","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Boundary, Relative Position, and Separation","parent_semantic_invariant":"Entities are located by edges, near and far relations, facing and trailing sides, and movement across a lower or outer boundary.","parent_surprising_reach":"Spatial language reaches from courtyards and distant places into spear anatomy, the human-facing side of an object, posteriority, and a lower bodily outlet.","key":"C","name":"Lower Boundary and Outward Separation","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A lower or rear boundary marks the point from which something is directed away and placed at a distance.","active_motifs":"lower bodily outlet `س ف ل:B007/m01`, active distancing `ب ع د:B003/m01`, limit crossed or reached `ط و ر:B003/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:2 `طور`; 95:5 `أسفل`، `سافلين`; 95:7 `بعد`","synthesis":"The lower outlet provides a concrete boundary, the limit gives it a terminal edge, and active distancing describes motion beyond that edge. The channel is organized by separation from a bounded body rather than by general bodily anatomy.","ayah_refs":["95:2","95:5","95:7"]},{"parent_channel":"Human Community, Rank, and Belonging","parent_semantic_invariant":"Human beings form provisioned groups structured by possession, labor, rank, kinship, protection, and intimate attachment.","parent_surprising_reach":"The human collective includes manual workers and travelers, distant kin, low rank, penetrating counsel, protective jealousy, a returned woman, and the inward circle of confidants.","key":"B","name":"Rank, Aptitude, and Counsel","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Social position is evaluated through low rank, practical aptitude, penetrating counsel, and failure of judgment.","active_motifs":"low social rank `س ف ل:B002/m01`, dullness and lagging capacity `ب ل د:B007/m01`, penetrating counsel `ب ع د:B008/m01`, weak opinion `ل ي س:B007/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:3 `البلد`; 95:5 `أسفل`، `سافلين`; 95:7 `بعد`; 95:8 `أليس`","synthesis":"Low rank and dull performance mark diminished standing, while penetrating counsel marks valued social capacity. Weak opinion reverses the latter, making hierarchy a relation of perceived usefulness as well as lineage or position.","ayah_refs":["95:3","95:5","95:7","95:8"]},{"parent_channel":"Human Community, Rank, and Belonging","parent_semantic_invariant":"Human beings form provisioned groups structured by possession, labor, rank, kinship, protection, and intimate attachment.","parent_surprising_reach":"The human collective includes manual workers and travelers, distant kin, low rank, penetrating counsel, protective jealousy, a returned woman, and the inward circle of confidants.","key":"C","name":"Kinship, Return, and Protective Honor","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A woman returns to her family, and kinship organizes protection, jealousy, guarded closure, and their dishonorable absence.","active_motifs":"distant kin `ب ع د:B005/m01`, returned or divorced woman `ر د د:B006/m01`, protective jealousy `غ ي ر:B004/m01`, guarded closure `خ ل ق:B012/m01`, dishonorable lack of jealousy `ل ي س:B008/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:4 `خلقنا`; 95:5 `رددناه`; 95:6 `غير`; 95:7 `بعد`; 95:8 `أليس`","synthesis":"Return places the woman back within her family network, where nearness and distance define responsibility. Protective jealousy and guarded closure express defense of that boundary, while the absence of jealousy marks its social inversion.","ayah_refs":["95:4","95:5","95:6","95:7","95:8"]},{"parent_channel":"Visible Appearance and Material Surface","parent_semantic_invariant":"A visible surface can display form, mark, polish, defect, decay, or ornament while concealing a different functional state.","parent_surprising_reach":"Human beauty reaches into pupil reflections, an intact but blind eye, facial marks, worn cloth, deceptive ornament, polished ground contact, and sealed smoothness.","key":"B","name":"Brow, Skin Mark, Beauty, and Facial Defect","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"The visible face is read through openness between the brows, marks on skin, beauty, and a defect that mixes ugliness with remaining form.","active_motifs":"clear space between the brows `ب ل د:B003/m01`, mark on skin or body `ب ل د:B006/m01`, facial or chin defect `ر د د:B008/m01`, visible beauty `ح س ن:B001/m01`","ayah_anchors":"95:3 `البلد`; ## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision. Add no bulk without a distinct
reader payoff, but do not compress a significant finding for brevity.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. They do not license recurrence, maturity, or a surah-wide
  channel name.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by related root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, developed through its final reader-visible result
   in proportion to its significance.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

Start each paragraph with reader meaning before grammar. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi
Allah'a verir; bunu fiille değil, sabit bir ad cümlesiyle yapar" over "Bu âyet,
tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik bir hüküm kurar."

Keep the prose anchored in this ayah's own wording without imposing a paragraph
or length quota. Give every significant finding enough space to make its
mechanism and reader payoff clear, even when it arrives through later context;
never compress it merely to keep the commentary short. Preserve every member of
the resonance set, and use a plain closing synthesis only when the ayah benefits
from one.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
confirm in friction that there was no live misconception, replacement risk, or
counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
